- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Hiawatha, leader of The Iroquois (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Iroquois
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mohawk Warrior, Replacing Swordsman (Requires technology: Iron Working)
        - Unique Unit: Prowler, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Tadodaho, Replacing Great Diplomat (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Longhouse, Replacing Herbalist (Requires technology: Calendar)
        - Civilization Ability: Units move through Forest and Jungle as if they were roads, and these tiles establish City Connections. Military Land Units start with the Woodsman Promotion. Gain 15% progress towards the next Tadodaho in your Capital when adopting a Social Policy.
      - Leader: Hiawatha
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      
      ## Policies
      - Transnationalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Transnationalism: Each turn, increases the chance that a Corporate Franchise will appear in a foreign City to 15% (normally 5%). Global Franchise maximum increased by 25%, and every owned Town you have built counts as a Franchise. +2 Culture from all Corporation Offices.
      - Universal Healthcare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Healthcare: Receive a free Hospital in every City. +50 when a Citizen is born in any City, scaling with Era.
      - Capitalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Capitalism: 2 Specialists in each of your cities generate +1 Happiness instead of -1 Unhappiness from Urbanization. Specialists generate +1 Gold and Science.
      - Their Finest Hour (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Their Finest Hour: All cities get +2 Air Unit Slots. Each Air Unit stationed in a city increases the City's Defense by 3. Can build B17 Bombers.
      - Arsenal of Democracy (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Arsenal of Democracy: +15% Production towards Military Units. +10 Influence with all known City-States when you expend Great People, and +40 Influence when you gift Units to City-States.
      - Draft Registration (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Draft Registration: Military Units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
      - Self Determination (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Self Determination: Liberating a city (including removing another civ's Sphere of Influence on a City-State through conquest) gives 15 XP to all units, 50 Influence with all City-States, and 40 Science, scaling with Era and City Population. The liberated city gains an Arsenal and 6 units.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Exchange Markets (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Trade Route.
        - * +1 Happiness for every active Trade Route.
        - * +15% Tourism modifier for Trade Routes.
        - * Resources from City-States count towards Global Monopolies.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

Increased DeceptiveBias to 5 - may need to appear cooperative while preparing desperate measures. Slightly increased FriendlyBias to 6, reduced HostileBias to 6 - creating minimal space for negotiation despite existential threat. Cannot declare wars (WarBias 1) but may need to respond to peace overtures. Nuclear option prepared but not committed.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Their Finest Hour (Policy)
        - Rationale: Turn 420: Their Finest Hour remains critical next policy, available in ~13 turns. Problem: French Cultural Victory in ~9 turns. Timeline doesn't work. However, B17s (85 ranged strength, 10 range) are the only units that could potentially strike French cities from distance. May be too late but must continue policy path. No better alternative - other Freedom tenets don't provide immediate military benefit.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Current relationship with India remains stable with 50/0 from previous turn. India is key defensive ally with Defensive Pact against France. Their combined military (875) plus mine (1435) = 2310 vs French 1608. They voted with us on Sanction France. Maintain strong public bond while private neutrality acknowledges separate religious identity (Hinduism) that may cause minor friction.
        - UpdatedTurn: 245
        
        ### Carthage
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Carthage war has 100% war score in my favor - no value in continuing. Need peace to focus all military resources on existential French threat (6834 military vs my 2561). Carthage only has Utique left (1 city, no capitals) and poses zero victory threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 328
        
        ### Assyria
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Carthage war has 100% war score in my favor with no value in continuing. They have only Utique remaining (now captured by France anyway). Setting neutral relationship to prepare for peace offer - need to eliminate this war to focus all resources on France. Carthage poses zero victory threat at this point.
        - UpdatedTurn: 333
        
        ### Arabia
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -40
        - Rationale: Turn 372: Arabia remains French vassal - war forced by French puppetmaster, not bilateral choice. Adjusting to negative reflects they're actively fighting me (war since forever), but acknowledging vassalization limits their agency. No peace possible as France prevents it. Will target their master instead of wasting effort on proxy.
        - UpdatedTurn: 372
        
        ### India
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Turn 393: India remains French vassal but continues trade routes with me (providing +60 science and gold). Their Mumbai military units are moving near my borders but positionally defending against Rome. India is already Influential to France (100%), so no further cultural damage from them. DELHI WAS CONVERTED TO PROTESTANTISM - minor victory against French Buddhism spread. Constitutionally cannot military support me. Maintaining positive economic relations for final turns - every beaker and gold matters when facing extinction.
        - UpdatedTurn: 394
        
        ### France
        - Public: -30
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Turn 420: France relationship adjusted. Setting moderate negative modifiers (-30/-50) instead of maximum hostility. This signals potential for negotiation despite ongoing war. France is winning via Cultural Victory in 9 turns - if they offer peace that slows their victory timer, I must be positioned to consider it. Underlying relationship remains hostile due to war and ideology, but this creates diplomatic flexibility for desperate peace attempts.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -70
        - Rationale: Turn 420: Rome relationship remains maximum hostile. They actively aid French victory by attacking me (destroyed my Dreadnought last turn, conducting naval operations near Montreal). Rome is the key to French Cultural Victory - when they become Influential, game ends. Rome is also at war with France, but their attacks on me help France. No negotiation possible with Rome while they collaborate in my extinction.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Turn 420: Poland relationship maintained positive. They remain the only non-Influential civilization voting against France (opposed Passport System). At 92% Influenced but still independent. Their Defensive Pact with France limits options, but positive relationship encourages continued World Congress resistance. Poland's spy was killed in my territory this turn (Akwesasme) - minor incident but maintaining relationship for strategic alignment against French cultural dominance.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Turn 421: EXTINCTION IN 9 TURNS - French Cultural Victory imminent when Rome becomes Influential (currently 89%). No meaningful strategic changes possible in this timeframe. Military situation deteriorating: lost Fleet Destroyer and AA Gun to Rome, French Tanks pillaging iron mines near Grand River. Conventional victory impossible. Nuclear option previously set to 50 remains last desperate hope, but likely insufficient. Must maintain current posture and hope for divine intervention or French peace overture. All plans contingent on surviving past turn 430.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 42
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 1 turn
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Iroquois Sphere of Influence: Huari:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.

            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Hiawatha, leader of The Iroquois (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Iroquois
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Mohawk Warrior, Replacing Swordsman (Requires technology: Iron Working)
        - Unique Unit: Prowler, Replacing Musketman (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Tadodaho, Replacing Great Diplomat (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Longhouse, Replacing Herbalist (Requires technology: Calendar)
        - Civilization Ability: Units move through Forest and Jungle as if they were roads, and these tiles establish City Connections. Military Land Units start with the Woodsman Promotion. Gain 15% progress towards the next Tadodaho in your Capital when adopting a Social Policy.
      - Leader: Hiawatha
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      
      ## Policies
      - Transnationalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Transnationalism: Each turn, increases the chance that a Corporate Franchise will appear in a foreign City to 15% (normally 5%). Global Franchise maximum increased by 25%, and every owned Town you have built counts as a Franchise. +2 Culture from all Corporation Offices.
      - Universal Healthcare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Universal Healthcare: Receive a free Hospital in every City. +50 when a Citizen is born in any City, scaling with Era.
      - Capitalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Capitalism: 2 Specialists in each of your cities generate +1 Happiness instead of -1 Unhappiness from Urbanization. Specialists generate +1 Gold and Science.
      - Their Finest Hour (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Their Finest Hour: All cities get +2 Air Unit Slots. Each Air Unit stationed in a city increases the City's Defense by 3. Can build B17 Bombers.
      - Arsenal of Democracy (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Arsenal of Democracy: +15% Production towards Military Units. +10 Influence with all known City-States when you expend Great People, and +40 Influence when you gift Units to City-States.
      - Draft Registration (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Draft Registration: Military Units purchased with Gold receive full XP.
      - Self Determination (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Self Determination: Liberating a city (including removing another civ's Sphere of Influence on a City-State through conquest) gives 15 XP to all units, 50 Influence with all City-States, and 40 Science, scaling with Era and City Population. The liberated city gains an Arsenal and 6 units.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Splendor (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Expending a Great Person grants 50 Culture, scaling with Era.
        - * State Treasury built in Capital (+4 Gold, +2 Culture to all Monuments, Gardens, and Baths; 1 Merchant Slot).
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Exchange Markets (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Trade Route.
        - * +1 Happiness for every active Trade Route.
        - * +15% Tourism modifier for Trade Routes.
        - * Resources from City-States count towards Global Monopolies.
      - Consulates (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.
        - * Completing a Trade Route to a City-State triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Industry (New Branch):
        - Industry provides bonuses to empires focused on Gold and Production.
        - Adopting Industry grants:
        - * +2 Trade Routes.
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Each Industry policy unlocked grants:
        - * -5% Gold needed for purchases.
        - Adopting all Policies in Industry grants:
        - * Unlocks building Broadway.
        - * +3 Happiness per unique owned Luxury Resource.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Specialists.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Merchants with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Refrigeration
        - Rationale: Set next technology to Refrigeration to progress towards late-game techs and unlock Offshore Platforms.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Select Their Finest Hour to bolster city defense and air combat capabilities, which is crucial since we are under heavy attack from France.
        - Policy: Their Finest Hour
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Private: 40
        - TargetID: 1
        - Rationale: Encourage peace with Rome to end our two-front war and allow us to focus our entire military strength on defending against France's massive army and stopping their imminent cultural victory.
        - Public: 40
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 30
        - TargetID: 4
        - Rationale: Maintain friendly and neutral relations with Poland to prevent them from declaring war on us, especially given their defensive pact with France.
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-persona
      - args:
        - WarBias: 8
        - Forgiveness: 8
        - DiplomaticBalance: 8
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 10
        - Rationale: Aggressively focus on defeating France (our main competitor) while being open to diplomatic peace with Rome to stabilize our eastern borders.
    - #5:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - Flavors:
          - Defense: 100
          - Air: 100
          - Diplomacy: 80
          - Production: 100
          - GreatPeople: 60
          - TileImprovement: 80
          - Gold: 60
          - Antiair: 100
          - Mobilization: 100
          - Offense: 100

        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Assessing Conquest Momentum**
            
            My focus is on evaluating our current military standing and victory path. We're deep into the Information Era, and the conquest strategy remains our primary objective. I'm actively assessing our war readiness and how we can best leverage our current position to achieve dominance.
            
            **Preventing Cultural Victory**
            
            I'm urgently assessing how to derail France's imminent Cultural Victory. They are on the cusp of influencing Rome and securing their win. My immediate priority is to find a way to disrupt their diplomatic and cultural dominance before they achieve victory.
            
            **Disrupting Cultural Victory**
            
            I'm intensely focused on preventing France's cultural win, which is alarmingly close. Rome is nearly under their sway. I'm exploring options, including World Congress resolutions, like repealing the Passport System, and considering how to directly impact France's cities or tourism output before they secure victory.
            
            **Analyzing War Scenarios**
            
            I'm currently assessing our multi-front war weariness and comparing military strengths. Despite our superior overall military power, our limited units and supply are concerning. I'm re-evaluating the impact of losing cities versus focusing on strategic strikes against France to curb their tourism.
            
            **Re-evaluating AI Priorities**
            
            I'm analyzing why our cities are prioritizing infrastructure over military production, despite our war focus. It appears our cities may have completed all available buildings, leading the AI to default to projects like Public Works. I need to investigate military unit production capabilities further.
            
            **Investigating Unit Production**
            
            I'm examining why military unit production is lagging. The AI's focus on "Infinite Project: Defense" and potential lack of demand for more units, despite ample supply, is puzzling. I'm now delving into strategic resource availability to see if that's a limiting factor.
            
            **Securing Strategic Resources**
            
            I've identified a critical shortage of strategic resources, particularly those vital for modern military units. With almost no aluminum, horses, or uranium, and limited coal and iron, our ability to build advanced units is severely hampered. I'm now investigating our oil situation and exploring potential trade partners, but our current conflicts severely limit options.
            
            **Seeking Strategic Resources**
            
            My immediate concern is the severe deficit in strategic resources, critically hindering modern unit creation. Poland's denouncement and defensive pact with France further complicates trade. I'm now investigating potential city-state alliances, specifically Huari and Quebec City, to alleviate this shortage and boost our diplomatic influence.
            
            **Securing City-State Allies**
            
            I'm prioritizing securing more city-state allies to access much-needed strategic resources. I'm reviewing available city-states and their resources, focusing on potential diplomatic gains. It seems I need to explore our own territory for undiscovered or unimproved Oil and Aluminum deposits.
            
            **Capturing Scarce Resources**
            
            I'm now examining player territories for crucial strategic resources. France and Rome possess abundant aluminum, coal, uranium, and horses, suggesting captured cities could solve our shortages. I'm specifically assessing Rome's former city of Montreal, now heavily defended, and the threat to our ally Huari.
            
            **Assessing Enemy Strength**
            
            I'm currently appraising the immense scale of France and Rome. France holds an astonishing 23 cities, and Rome an even larger 31. Despite my own empire's modest size, our military strength is surprisingly robust, packed with advanced units, yet we're precariously situated between these two behemoths who are actively warring against me.
            
            **Analyzing Global Conflict**
            
            The realization that Rome and France are also at war with each other, in addition to our own ongoing conflicts, drastically alters the strategic landscape. This three-way global conflict presents a complex web of shifting alliances and potential opportunities to disrupt France's looming cultural victory.
            
            **Liberating Montreal**
            
            I'm re-evaluating the war with Rome. They've captured my city, Montreal, and are currently at war with France, which is key. My focus is now on liberating Montreal; achieving this might reset our relations with Rome, especially if they're losing the war against France. It's a potential path to peace with Rome, allowing me to fully concentrate on France's cultural victory.
            
            **Recapturing Key Territory**
            
            I'm assessing the possibility of retaking Montreal, noting the Roman defenses. However, France's cultural victory remains the paramount threat, with only nine turns left to influence Rome and achieve their win condition. I'm now scrutinizing their victory requirements, particularly the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
            
            **Targeting Tourist Cities**
            
            I need to disrupt France's tourism by capturing their high-value cities like Paris, Lyon, Orleans, or Tours. Alternatively, eliminating France entirely or recapturing their capitals are options, but less feasible with only nine turns remaining until their cultural victory. I'm also considering if Rome's war with France offers any leverage.
            
            **Analyzing Border Defenses**
            
            I'm assessing the substantial French military buildup near our borders, specifically around Grand River and Huari. Their reported strength in Zone 1704 seems disproportionate to the visible units, suggesting reinforcements from adjacent territories like Hippo Regius or Huari are likely.
            
            **Defending Strategic Locations**
            
            I'm intensely focused on the critical defense of our western border, specifically the Grand River and Huari. Huari's current precarious state, at 0% health, is alarming; its potential fall to France opens a direct path to Onondaga and Osininka, which they would annex. Given France's imminent cultural victory and massive assault, maximizing our combat effectiveness and damaging France is paramount. I'm also assessing research, noting Stealth is 8 turns away, unlocking powerful Stealth Bombers, while Refrigeration is the next option.
            
            **Selecting Next Research**
            
            My immediate focus is on determining our next technological pursuit. With Stealth nearing completion, I'm setting Refrigeration as the subsequent research target, ensuring our progress continues efficiently as it's the only current option.
            
            **Choosing Tenets Wisely**
            
            I'm evaluating our current social policies to maximize our advantage. "Their Finest Hour" is beneficial for defense, but "Arsenal of Democracy" might boost military production more. I'm also considering "Universal Healthcare" and "Capitalism," but our current happy status and conquest goal make the military-focused tenets more appealing.
            
            **Assessing Defensive Pacts**
            
            I'm analyzing Poland's defensive pact with France and how it impacts our ongoing war. Given we're already engaged with France, I need to confirm if Poland has joined the conflict due to this pact, especially since we've also denounced each other.
            
            **Clarifying Alliance Status**
            
            I'm clarifying Poland's defensive pact with France. It appears their pact didn't trigger our current war because France likely declared war on us first. This is crucial because Poland isn't currently at war with me, and I need to maintain that neutrality.
            
            **Negotiating Peace with Rome**
            
            I'm actively working to foster a more positive relationship with Rome, aiming for a peace treaty. By adjusting our public and private relationship modifiers, I hope to persuade the AI to accept terms, allowing us to redirect our full military might towards halting France's cultural victory.
            
            **Adjusting Persona Parameters**
            
            I'm tweaking our internal parameters to reflect a more aggressive stance towards France, our primary competitor, while simultaneously enhancing our capacity for peaceful resolutions with other civilizations like Rome. This adjustment aims to optimize our strategic approach for both offensive operations and diplomatic flexibility.
            
            **Improving Resource Acquisition**
            
            I'm prioritizing improving our Tile Improvement and Infrastructure flavors to 80 to secure vital strategic resources. With Refineries in development and limited existing coal, I'm focusing on improving our ability to discover and exploit any available coal deposits to meet our modern unit needs.
            
            **Adjusting AI Flavors**
            
            I'm recalibrating the AI's strategic priorities. Diplomacy and Gold flavors are being significantly boosted to secure essential city-state alliances and their strategic resources, while Spaceship and Science are slightly reduced to maintain focus on our primary conquest objective against France.
            
            **Setting Diplomatic Parameters**
            
            I am now adjusting our diplomatic settings to facilitate peace with Rome and maintain neutrality with Poland. Concurrently, I'm fine-tuning our AI flavors to prioritize diplomacy and gold acquisition, bolstering city-state alliances and resource procurement while keeping our conquest focus sharp.
            
            **Refining Diplomatic Posture**
            
            I am now adjusting our diplomatic relationships to secure peace with Rome and maintain neutrality with Poland. My focus is on boosting diplomacy and gold acquisition flavors to strengthen city-state alliances and resource procurement, all while keeping our conquest of France paramount.

        - #3:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: s5fCorSuZI4NWG8N

          - input:

            - TargetID: 1

          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #4:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: L8ZTjOKyLtIW9He6
          - toolName: set-relationship
          - input:
            - Public: 40
            - Private: 30
            - TargetID: 4

          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #5:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: TiZpiN20t0MLMUN3
